# Onboarding and trust: signed events, the public instance, links (design, 2026-11-15)

> **Status (2026-11-16):** this design is being built on the integration branch. A rule the code does
> not keep yet is marked "Not built yet" or "Not kept yet" in its own section, with the date. Nothing
> of it is released.
>
> **Owner decisions:** recorded 2026-11-14 (§0) and authoritative. Where this document conflicts with
> specs 01–05, this document wins from the cut-over on, and the specs are rewritten to match it (§15).
>
> **Clean break:** there is **no compatibility period**. The new formats replace the old ones in one
> cut-over. Nothing here reads, migrates or tolerates a pre-cut-over format, and the store apps never
> contain one.

## Review amendments

Two reviews of the first draft (2026-11-14), both GO-WITH-AMENDMENTS. Every item was fixed; none was declined.
- The coherence review (2026-11-14): ids B and R.
- The threat review (2026-11-14): ids T-B and T-R.
- Owner directions of 2026-11-14: ids O.
- Fixes the threat review asked for to amendments it saw before they were committed: ids U.
- The coherence re-review of the amended draft (2026-11-14): ids C2.
- The threat re-review of the amended draft (2026-11-14): ids T2. Where an item was already closed by a C2 amendment, the row says so and names the section that answers it.
- The two final reviews before the freeze (2026-11-14): ids FS (security reader) and FI (interface reader). FX marks a contradiction found while amending.
- The closing review (2026-11-15): ids FC.
- The notes that came with the freeze of IF-1 and IF-2 (2026-11-15): ids FN. None changes a format, a route or a vector.
- The questions raised while spec 03 was written (2026-11-15), where the design was silent or said two things: ids S3. Each settles a rule for spec 03; none changes a signed format or a vector.
- The independent review of spec 03 (2026-11-15): ids SR.
- The questions raised while the test vectors were written (2026-11-15), where a verdict, an order or a grammar edge had more than one reading: ids VR. Two decisions taken with them: ids IN.

| Id | What changed | Where |
|---|---|---|
| B1 | The instance runs single-origin. The break-glass twin origin (`ANDVARI_PUBLIC_HOSTNAME` and every refusal keyed to it) is deleted. Session, refresh and two-step rules are restated for an internet-facing instance. | §7.8, §15 (server, spec 03, clients) |
| B2 | `leave` is allowed while a vault is deleted. A deletion builds leaves at the served head for every vault where the account holds a non-owner grant, adopted or not. Operator deletion needs no leaves (owners later sign `remove`). Leaves are exempt from the `vault_recovery` bucket, and the body has a cap. | §3.3, §3.11, §9.2 |
| B3 | A `delete` keeps members in the log and keeps their fetch access; `restore` re-grants them. Only `remove` and `leave` cut a member off. | §3.8 |
| B4 | Adds a test mail sink (test mode only); host-minted `enrol` links (`andvari admin enrol-link`, unsigned like setup) for tests, demos and seeding; a controllable test clock. The e2e, uitest, live-e2e and recovery-drill scripts are rewritten by a new part of the build, tests, CI and the hostile-server harness. | §6.4, §7.1, §9.5, §11.1, §18 (tests, CI and the hostile-server harness) |
| B5 | The extension gets a reduced rule. It trusts only UVK wraps, verifies the instance card and the account genesis, and writes only to the personal vault; it verifies no logs. The card now names the operator's **Ed25519** key, so tweetnacl alone can check the chain. The extension's work resized to M; it waits for IF-1. | §4.1, §4.2, §12.2, §18 |
| R1 | Every file now belongs to one part of the build. A new part, tests, CI and the hostile-server harness, takes scripts, CI, test seeds and the harness (proxy, mail sink, test clock). The core takes `core/src/iosMain`. | §18.2 |
| R2 | IF-3 is the first deliverable of the core session and app API: an API-only commit with fakes, merged before work on the Android, iPhone and desktop apps starts. It has a complete list and done-when rules for every interface. | §18.1 |
| R3 | Byte encodings are pinned: token hash, `nPub`, MAC and proof strings, platform values, proof-of-work fields, `emailNorm`, bundle field types (now `"v":2`), how an opened offer is recognised and matched to the server's kind, kit QR padding, and a code-point list for `name` that depends on no Unicode tables. | §2.2, §2.3, §6.4, §8.3, §10 |
| R4 | The origin grammar is the single source. Setup and the Trust Gate refuse any origin it cannot carry. Plain-http LAN setups are dropped (phones refuse them anyway). | §2.2, §9.5, §12.3 |
| R5 | `PUT /escrow/self` is kept only for re-sealing after a **rotation statement signed offline by the outgoing recovery seed**. Any other change of the recovery key needs the person's password re-consent (revised by T-B4). | §4.5 |
| R6 | Removal-list gaps closed: `isAdmin` becomes `role`; `mustChangePassword`; `recoveryFingerprint` and `userAttachmentsMaxBytes` in the client policy; `GET /users/{id}/identity`; every `/admin/users/*` route; `EnrollCeremony.kt`; the extension's personal-change code, `trustmigrate.ts` and the enrol-link origin regex. | §9.2, §15 |
| R7 | The environment is the source of truth. `andvari admin signup …` edits it and restarts. The panel can only tighten, and the tighter value wins. | §7.1, §9.5 |
| R8 | Adds a durable mail outbox with retries, a stall metric and alert, and an operator banner. While mail is stalled, sign-up and recovery requests answer `503 mail_unavailable` (uniform). The plan's notice cap is fixed to 20 a day. | §7.2, §11.4 |
| R9 | Cut-over gains: a rollback (keep the old app on one device until the new instance is verified), wording without "read-only", a quota override before the import, `vault_create` pacing, draining the iOS save queue, the wipe of old local state on first launch, and one line on other accounts. | §14.1, §12.4 |
| R10 | Losing the operator is now written down: a new instance identity and re-baked store builds. Adds resume after a crash before card 0 is uploaded, and the server's checks that card 0 names the operator account. | §4.3, §4.6, §9.5 |
| R11 | Demo accounts come from host `enrol` links run on the server host: no mail, no operator session elsewhere. They count against the cap. The CLI gains `card sign`. | §9.5, §18.2 |
| R12 | Device links get a state machine. A new link replaces an open one. Adds a cancel route and a 5-minute pickup window; a session the new device never collects is revoked, and the new-device email is sent at pickup. | §8.2 |
| R13 | Added dead-end flows: a link for another instance; a link reaching a server not taking accounts. Families get first-class private instances: members create register links (so each member is pinned to the person who invited them), and the recovery copy is written for families. **The operator account stays separate everywhere** (revised by T-B4 / U2). Moving off the public instance is spelled out. | §6.6, §6.7, §7.7, §9.1, §14.4 |
| R14 | Smaller fixes: the genesis for offline unlock comes from the cached AccountKeys; the link record is saved before posting (in device state since T-B6, so no merge rule is needed); `add` checks membership counts; status codes and error names deduplicated; two new email templates. | §5.2, §6.7, §6.9, §3.7, §11.3 |
| Rec. | Parts of the build resized (the core XL and split, the server XL, the iPhone app L, the extension M). `add` and `role` get a bucket. Frozen vaults refuse more writes. Quota refusals are per mutation. The kit QR carries `card0Hash`. Phones can paste links. Purge is distinguished from withholding. Plan drift fixed. | §3.7, §3.9, §7.4, §10, §12.1, §3.8, §18 |
| T-B1 | The comparison codes are now **committed three-pass** codes: commit, exchange, reveal. Neither side can grind them. The device-link bundle travels under a **UVK-derived key** and opens only after the password. | §2.3, §6.11, §8.2, §8.3 |
| T-B2 | Vaults attached to a link are shared **only after the codes matched** (`compared = true`) or after a per-vault release behind a warning. The rules for `compared = false` contacts are defined. | §6.7 step 8, §6.10 |
| T-B3 | Shared-vault UVK wraps carry the **genesis hash in their AD**, and the account keeps per-vault `{genesisHash, head}` records in account state that its devices cross-check. | §2.3, §3.6 (d), (e) |
| T-B4 | Recovery consent lives in **UVK-sealed account state**; clients never act on a served posture. No silent re-seal: a key rotation needs a **statement signed offline by the outgoing recovery seed** (new purpose 0x08), and anything else needs password re-consent. The operator **never signs in on the web**. Both variants for the public instance (K: kept and hardened; R: removed) are designed. | §4.5, §7.7, §9.1 |
| T-B5 | Assisted recovery gains a **7-day hold with a veto from every signed-in device**, a **96-bit request code** from `rPub` that the CLI checks, a payload **signed by the recovery key** (new purpose 0x09), and copy saying whoever controls the email can ask. Per-address denial is recorded as a residual. | §2.3, §9.3, R27 |
| T-B6 | The link secret **stays on the creating device**: no account state, so no web exposure. A **creator fingerprint `F`** in the fragment must match the offer's key. | §6.1, §6.7, §6.8 |
| T-R1 | `at` never decreases along a log. An owner entry dated after expiry clears the offer, and accept is bounded by both. | §3.3 |
| T-R2 | The server runs the **shared core verifier**. Clients retry the **stored frame byte for byte**, keeping a pending-append record. | §3.7 |
| T-R3 | The creator drops its record at expiry or revoke, and late answers are ignored whatever the server says. | §6.7 step 9, §6.8 |
| T-R4 | Incoming vaults need a **one-time accept naming the owner** (this replaces Q5's auto-adopt). The server refuses `add` without an answered link between the pair. | §3.6, §3.7 |
| T-R5 | Checks added: `from` and key against the server-reported creator and `F`; a second key for a pinned userId needs a compared code; labels show only for matching keys; contacts made on other devices become current with one tap (G3). | §6.6, §6.8, §6.9, §3.9 |
| T-R6 | Overclaims corrected: G7 honest; operator recovery lets the operator act as the member; web account-state writes; a removed member plus the server can read **and write**, with "Move everything to a new vault"; a withheld `add` understates members. | §1, §3.13, §7.7, §13, R3, R7, R21 |
| T-R7 | The compromise story: store builds ignore card-driven moves of the public instance; no account re-key exists, so a compromise means starting fresh; the operator key's limits are stated. | §4.4, R30, R33 |
| T-R8 | The product origin is a named residual. Card names are always shown with their origin. | §4.4, R32 |
| T-R9 | The new device's digits are **typed** on the existing device. The session exists only **after a password proof**. The bundle is a listed card root (since T2-B1 it only confirms the pin made before the claim). | §8.2, §4.4 |
| T-R10 | Operator deletion is defined without leaves (amends spec 03 §13's "no admin deletes"). Disabled accounts **do not** count against the cap. `card0Hash` is taken from the signing device. | §3.11, §7.4, §14.1 |
| T-Rec | Writers put the vault's log head in items (gossip). The account ID appears in every notice. | §3.9, §11.2 |
| U1 | The no-reauth re-seal after quick unlock was removed (see T-B4). | §4.5 |
| U2 | Default P no longer lets the operator be an everyday account. | §0.2, §9.1 |
| U3 | A host-minted enrol link is **not** a card root. It gets the typed-address Trust Gate (the baked root on the public instance). | §4.4, §6.8 |
| U4 | An operator reset keeps the **instance id** and starts a new chain, so genesis checks still pass and clients see a fork. | §4.6 |
| O1 | The domain is decided: `https://andvaripass.com` (site, join pages, association files), the instance at `https://vault.andvaripass.com`, display name "andvari", mail from **`support@andvaripass.com`**. One shared mailbox both sends and receives; it gets send-only Graph access, and no separate no-reply address is needed. | §0.3, §11.1 |
| O2 | The Android application id changes at the cut-over (to `com.andvaripass.app`, decided on 2026-11-15) in both flavors. The visible name, the R namespace and every iOS identifier stay (the iOS identifiers were decided anew on 2026-11-24, as §0.3 says). The Android signing key was an open item, settled on 2026-11-16 (§0.6). | §0.3, §12.1, §12.4, §14.1, §15, §18 (the Android app) |
| O3 | Only the owner's everyday account is carried across at the cut-over; the test account on the old server is not. | §14.1 |
| C2-X1 | `add` checks a durable `connections(a, b)` row instead of link rows. The row is written when a connect link is answered **or** an account registers through a register link, the janitor never touches it, and it goes only with an account. | §3.6, §3.7, §3.11, §6.6 |
| C2-N1 | The extension reads `vaults[vaultId].genesisHash` from account state to open genesis-bound wraps; a wrong value just fails the AEAD. | §12.2 |
| C2-N2 | `reset-instance-identity` demotes the old operator in the same transaction and is the named exception to "setup only with no accounts". A kit whose `card0Hash` doesn't match falls back to the Trust Gate with a warning. | §4.4, §4.6, §7.3, §9.5 |
| C2-N3 | Adds: purpose 0x0A `recovery-veto` with its template; `GET /instance` serves `rotations`, with `PUT /instance/rotation` to upload them; `reveal` is public (it is bound by the commitment); device-link pickup takes `totp`; grammar type `email_b64n`. | §2.1, §2.2, §4.3, §6.4, §6.6, §8.2, §9.3 |
| C2-N4 | The vector plan matches the grammar (no LAN http, no NFC) and the four-part fragment. | §17 |
| C2-N5 | `VaultLog.verify` and the other pure verifiers move to the crypto and statement verifiers, and the server depends on them. Platform actuals are split by package (crypto to the verifiers, client to the core engine, the iOS bridge to the core session and app API). Test sets, `settings.gradle.kts`, `Dockerfile`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `policy-clamps.test.ts` and the rest of `web/src` are owned. The assetlinks server test moves to the server row. | §15, §18.2, §18.3 |
| C2-N6 | Merge rules for the account-state records `vaults`, `accepted` and `recovery`, and the key-change rule for `contacts`. | §5.4 |
| C2-N7 | Permanent mail failures are dropped at once. "Stalled" means a non-empty queue with no successful send for 10 minutes. | §11.4 |
| C2-N8 | `andvari admin signup …` also clears the database's tighter overrides. | §7.1, §9.5 |
| C2-N9 | `andvari setup` also prints an enrol link for the runner's everyday account. Operator views live in the desktop app and `andvari-cli operator`, never on the web or phones, so the phone apps do not take setup links either. Operator recovery requires mail. A phones-only family's host-held operator key is recorded under R30. | §9.1, §9.5, §14.4, §18.1, R30 |
| C2-N10 | The certificate paths are named (caddy HTTP-01 or DNS-01, tunnel, `tailscale serve`, own proxy). Android UI tests use `adb reverse`. | §9.5, §18.2 (the specification and documents; tests, CI and the hostile-server harness) |
| C2-N11 | Variant K's stranger channel is described honestly. `rSec` and `pollSecret` persist for the 7-day hold. | §9.3 |
| C2-N12 | Kit recovery and assisted recovery clear two-step sign-in and send `two_step_off`. | §9.3, §10 |
| C2-N13 | A grant revoked by `vault_delete` counts for `leave`. | §3.7 |
| C2-B4 | `andvari setup --test` is defined. | §9.5 |
| C2-R2 | IF-3 gains accept and decline, the key-change prompt, the per-device contact prompt, the veto and re-consent prompts, "Move everything", "Remove from the list", instance switching and the first-launch wipe. Operator views are desktop and CLI only. | §18.1 |
| C2-R11 | `andvari-cli` connects by the origin's own address; it no longer dials loopback under the public origin's name (owner, 2026-11-15: no extra HTTP library). | §9.5 |
| C2-Rec | In person, both people type each other's code. A decline signs a `leave`. Unaccepted and frozen vaults stay out of AutoFill and the extension. A rollback loses new data. "Move everything" needs free quota. The copy names the other person's device. §12.3 and §12.4 are reordered. The cut-over needs two devices or the CLI. | §6.7, §6.11, §3.6, §3.13, §14.1, §12 |
| T2-B1 | A device link no longer sends the password to an address nothing vouches for. The new device accepts a `/d` link only on its own "Link from your other device" screen (`/d` is no longer an app link), pins the link's origin **before it claims** (baked root, kit or setup, or the Trust Gate, now or earlier), names the origin on the password screen, and then requires the bundle's card checkpoint to confirm that pin. This is one case of a new general **password rule**: a value derived from an account's secrets goes only to the origin of an existing session for that account, or to an origin with a password pin; a pin that came only from someone else's link needs the Trust Gate first. | §0.3, §4.4, §6.1, §6.7, §8.1, §8.2, §8.4, §12.1, §12.3, §16 (T12, R22), §17 (`passwordgate.json`), §18 |
| T2-R-a | The server refuses a log entry whose `at` is more than 10 minutes ahead of its own clock (`400 at_ahead`). The shared verifier stays clock-free. Heads served for building entries carry `at`, including the deletion preview's. | §3.3, §3.7, §3.8, §3.11, §17 |
| T2-R-b | Code nonces are single-assignment. E fixes the claim when it draws `nonceE`; N computes its code from the `nonceE` it held when it revealed; the server stores each nonce and each reveal once; any change cancels. | §6.6, §6.11, §8.2 |
| T2-R-c | Already closed: the veto is purpose 0x0A with its template, and device-link pickup takes `totp` (C2-N3). New: the panel and the CLI never display the request code, so the operator must get it from the person. The poll secret moves from the URL into a POST body (recommended). | §9.2, §9.3 |
| T2-R-d | The merge rules were added by C2-N6. Added: a missing record never blocks adoption, and a record only ever refuses. Any holder of the UVK can write a record that bricks a vault for that account (new residual R35). | §3.6 (e), §5.4, R35 |
| T2-R-e | A device seals or re-seals an escrow without asking only when its **own** mirror of the recovery record agrees: set on this device, or confirmed by a device-link checkpoint. A device without one asks, and sealing needs the master password. | §4.5 |
| T2-R-f | Recovery copy names the instance and origin from the pinned card, never a server-reported operator name. An unsigned recovery-key change defaults to turning operator recovery off and says it may mean a compromise. R30 corrected. | §4.5, §7.7, R30 |
| T2-R-g | Already closed by C2-X1: the durable `connections(a, b)` row, written when a connect link is answered or an account registers through a register link, and never removed by the janitor. Every contact in this design comes from one of those two links (§6.9), so no older kind of contact exists. | §3.6, §3.7, §6.6 |
| T2-R-h | A purge keeps the vault's log with its tombstone. "Deleted for good" needs a verified owner-signed `delete` at the tombstone's head; anything else is withholding. A device's own copy moves to the 30-day holding area, never straight to deletion. | §3.8, §3.11 |
| T2-R-i | Already closed by C2-Rec and the follow-up commit: `compared` is set only by this device's own typed match. Now stated as a rule: no server flag or message from the other app can set it. | §6.11 |
| T2-R-j | The extension section states what a hostile server can and cannot do to an extension-only session, that the extension never seals an escrow (it cannot verify 0x08), and that store builds never follow a move or `kind` change of the baked public instance. The `genesisHash` source was added by C2-N1. | §12.2 |
| T2-R-k | IF-3's operator views (C2-R2) and the public `reveal` (C2-N3) were already fixed. New: copying items **out** of a frozen vault is allowed, so "Move everything" works there too, and it signs nothing on the frozen log. | §3.6, §3.13 |
| T2-T-R6 | §9.2's "cannot" list, the one-tap list's copy and R23 now say what a holder of the UVK can do: the operator of an operator-recovery account, and a hostile web bundle. | §8.5, §9.2, R23 |
| T2-T-R5 | `method` has the single value `"link"`. `"setup"` is not a value, and decoders refuse it. | §6.9 |
| T2-Rec | The remote comparison is typed too. Under variant R, `kind = public` means no escrow whatever `recoveryFp` says. In both variants a `kind` change needs the Trust Gate, and store builds never follow one for the public instance. `pollSecret` leaves the URL. Both variants are written out to be built, with what remains under K and what gets awkward under R. | §4.4, §6.11, §7.7, §9.3, §12.2 |
| FS-B1 | A kit is accepted only on Sign in → Forgot your master password? → Scan your recovery kit (new entry `kit_recovery_screen`); "Scan an invite" refuses kit payloads. The recovery screen names the account and server. Kit and setup pins count only for their own account and flow. The kit and assisted recovery commits carry `newMemberRecovery`, so the old kit dies at once and a new one is forced. | §4.4, §8.2 step 0, §9.3 step 5, §10, §12.1, §17 (`passwordgate.json`), §18.1 |
| FS-R1 | Chose to **strike** the checkpoint clause rather than carry E's mirror in the bundle. Reason: the linking device may itself have no mirror (the attack starts on a password-only phone), so a carried mirror would need a new field plus a rule for when E's own mirror counts, for little gain. The cost is one prompt per linked device, and only when a seal is due (a rotation). | §4.5, §8.3 |
| FS-R2 | First consent walks the card chain from the pin root; any `recoveryFp` change without a matching 0x08 shows the takeover warning with "Only me" preselected. R30 and the host-signing note in §9.5 corrected. | §4.5, §9.5 step 7, R30, §17 (`recovery.json`) |
| FS-R3 | "Move everything" on a frozen or failed vault re-adds only members the owner ticks, none by default. | §3.13, §18.1 |
| FS-R4 | An item's `logHead` is a hint to fetch, never a verdict; a fork needs two validly signed entries. | §3.9 |
| FS-R5 | No small fix. Recorded in "What remains under K", in §9.3 and in R27, with what it means on a family server (the operator's own channel still works). Verify attempts were capped at 10 per address per day across requests; the second server review replaced that with the per-request cap alone (§9.3). | §7.7, §9.3, R27, Appendix A |
| FS-R6 | `accepted` binds to the `add` it answers (`addSeq`); a re-add after a leave or remove asks again. | §3.6, §5.4, §17 (`accountstate.json`), §18.1 |
| FS-R7 | From an unverified head, a deletion refuses a `head.at` more than a day ahead of the device clock. | §3.11 |
| FS-C1 | The purge text no longer claims the server "cannot produce" deleted-for-good: it can withhold a restore, which is why copies go to the holding area. | §3.8 |
| FI-B1 | An undefined signer key is `precondition`; expired-offer clearing runs before step 5. | §3.3, §17 (`vaultlog.json`) |
| FI-B2 | One table maps every verdict and server check to its wire status and code. | §3.7 |
| FI-B3 | Log paging defined: `seq > after`, no `after` starts at genesis, repeat to `head.seq`. | §3.8 |
| FI-B4 | `GET /links/{id}` answers `200` for answered links, so the answerer gets `reveal`; `link_used` only on answer and register. | §6.6 |
| FI-B5 | Register's auth keys: `auth.link`, `auth.enrol`, `auth.setup`, each matching its kind. | §6.6 |
| FI-B6 | Device-link responses defined (E's GET, N's poll); `outer` and `approveMac` reach N through the poll; `proofHash` lets the server check `proof`; reveal takes `{nonceN, proof}`. | §8.2, §17 |
| FI-B7 | `GET /account/recovery-requests` and a `/sync` count tell a device which request to veto. | §9.3 step 2 |
| FI-B8 | An enrol link's gate records root `enrol`, which is not a password pin. | §4.4, §17 |
| FI-B9 | Setup is gated on "no operator account"; every other registration waits for card 0 (`409 setup_incomplete`); the everyday enrol link is minted at step 8; `--test` finishes setup headlessly. | §6.4, §6.7, §7.3, §9.5 |
| FI-B10 | Kit parsers ignore spaces and line breaks only; dashes are base64url. | §10, §17 |
| FI-B11 | W1 (variant R on the public instance) and W2 (Q4 yes) recorded as working assumptions awaiting the owner; §0.1's sign-up promise amended; IF-2 written for R; K is Appendix A's bounded delta. | §0.1, §7.7, §9.2, §18.1, §19, Appendix A |
| FI-B12 | `/d` fragments have exactly three parts; parse rows added. | §8.1, §17 |
| FI-B13 | 0x09 field types given. | §9.3 step 3, §17 |
| FI-B14 | `accountState.seq` is the in-blob counter, not the served CAS token. | §5.4, §8.3, §17 |
| FI-R1 | `PUT /escrow/self` takes `rotations[]`. | §4.5, §17 |
| FI-R2 | The † proof travels in the JSON body; the escrow read becomes a `POST`; the † on policy is on the `PUT` only. | §4.3, §9.2 |
| FI-R3 | `PUT /instance/rotation` added to §9.2; `PUT /instance/card` refusal codes; `GET /recovery-pubkey` named and in R's `404` list. | §4.3, §7.7, §9.2 |
| FI-R4 | Register refusals for steps 1–9; `pow` carries `{date, nonce}`. | §7.2, §7.3, §17 |
| FI-R5 | `PUT /account/recovery` carries the sealed account state with `baseSeq`. | §7.7 |
| FI-R6 | Claim `name` is the `name` type; the answer envelope is capped at 2732 characters. | §6.6, §8.2 step 2 |
| FI-R7 | A leave in the grace period rewrites the revocation reason to `left`, so restore skips it. | §3.8 |
| FI-R8 | `accepted` has one shape; `compared` merges as OR; `accountstate.json` covers §5.4. | §3.6, §5.4, §17, §18.1 |
| FI-R9 | Code and request lifetimes, poll states before fulfilment, and the veto's session authentication. | §9.3 |
| FI-R10 | **`deviceSecret` is in IF-2 now**, on every device-creating call, with the require-link route; only the Settings switch may come later. Reason: collected from the first device on, turning requireLink on never strands a device or reopens a frozen body. | §7.3, §8.2 step 7, §8.7, §9.3, §10 |
| FI-R11 | The offer window is `400 bad_expiry`; access (grant, or vault id for genesis) comes before the head check. | §3.7 |
| FI-C1 | §15's Android row lists `/l` only. | §15 |
| FI-C2 | §16.3 retires R14(a), (d), (e), (f), (g), as §15 deletes them. | §16.3 |
| FI-C3 | The extension grades no link or kit rows. | §17 |
| FI-L1 | The core engine's first commit (Wire DTOs, `EnrollLink` deletion) is a stated predecessor of the server and of the Android, iPhone and desktop apps. | §18.2, §18.3 |
| FI-L2 | `spec/public-instance.json`, part of the specification, holds the baked constants. | §18.1, §18.2 |
| FI-L3 | §15 credits deletions in `vector-gen`, `recovery-cli` and `bringup.sh` to the specification and its vectors, and to the server. | §15 |
| FI-L4 | TypeScript verifiers with the crypto and statement verifiers (`web/src/crypto/trust`); web adoption and merge in the web app (`web/src/vault`). | §18.2 |
| FI-L5 | The CLI lists `operator enrol` and `card show`. | §18.2 |
| FI-K1, FI-K2 | The false and conflicting code claims are fixed by FI-B10 and FI-R7. | §10, §3.8 |
| FX-1 | §3.7 said log rows go at the purge, against T2-R-h's year-long tombstone log. | §3.7 |
| FC-1 | A byte-identical frame signed by the caller answers `200` before the grant check; a `vault_delete` revocation counts for the owner's `restore` and `delete` and for `leave`. New server vectors `logappend.json`. | §3.7, §17 |
| FC-2 | The first-consent walk starts at card 0 for every pin root, with what anchors card 0 per root and what a pin made on an already-compromised server cannot detect. §9.5, R30 and `recovery.json` match. | §4.5, §9.5, R30, §17 |
| FC-3 | `approveMac` covers `outer` only, so it can be checked before the password; `inner` stays bound by its AEAD under the UVK-derived key and its link AD. Rejected: returning `inner` with the poll (it would leave before the password proof), and a MAC over `outer ‖ SHA-256(inner)` (a new field and a second check; it would also have tied the bundle to the QR secret, which matters only against a UVK holder working with the server, recorded under R35, FN-3). | §8.2, §17 |
| FC-4 | A reset deletes the stored cards and rotations; `GET /instance` serves the setup block until the new card 0; the stale "named exception" sentence is gone. | §4.6, §9.5 |
| FC-5 | The janitor expires only open rows; an answered link answers `200` until deleted. | §6.6 |
| FC-6 | Recovery verify answers a uniform `400 bad_code`. | §9.3 |
| FC-7 | Adopting through a self-wrap or owner wrap also needs an `accepted` record for the latest `add`. | §3.6 (d) |
| FC-8 | A revoke clears the device's secret hash; `link_required` comes only after the password verifies. | §8.6, §8.7 |
| FC-9 | A kit scanned in the wrong place gets "This is a recovery kit, not an invite." with no directions. | §10, §12.1 |
| FC-10 | Adopted: while an item-head hint is unresolved, the member list is marked "may be incomplete". | §3.9 |
| FN-1 | The Chrome and Firefox extension opens a shared-vault wrap only when `accepted[vaultId].decision = accept`; it cannot check `addSeq` (R24). §3.6 no longer claims such vaults are "never offered" to that extension. | §3.6, §12.2 rule 1 |
| FN-2 | The genesis-owner adoption path applies only while the log has no `add` of that account. | §3.6 (d) |
| FN-3 | R35 records what a UVK holder working with the server gains from `approveMac` covering `outer` alone; FC-3's rejection reason and §8.2 are softened to match. | R35, §8.2, FC-3 row |
| S3-Q1 | Every route has a rate bucket, with the numbers named; unnamed authenticated routes get 60 reads a minute and 60 writes an hour per account. | §7.8 |
| S3-Q2 | Login sends `{platform, name, deviceSecret, deviceId?}`; a device is known only when both id and secret match a live device, and anything else is a new device, refused under `requireLink`. | §8.7 |
| S3-Q3 | `400 email_domain_blocked`, checked before any account lookup. | §7.2 |
| S3-Q4 | `platform` is a body field on the recovery request, shown as self-reported. | §9.3 step 1 |
| S3-Q5 | The panel's sign-up controls sit in the policy; looser values get `400 not_tighter`. | §7.1 |
| S3-Q6 | Client-set expiries get 10 minutes of slack on the upper bound; server timers get none. | §7.8 |
| S3-Q7 | Re-enabling an account ignores the cap. | §7.4 |
| S3-Q8, S3-Q9 | `WireVault`'s transfer and deletion fields and `AccountKeys.escrowFingerprint` are removed, with no hints in their place. | §15 |
| S3-C1 | `email_taken` and `user_id_taken` are `409`. | §7.3 |
| S3-C2 | `PUT /account/personal-wrap` is deleted. | §5.3, §15 |
| S3-C3 | A new device link cancels every other link not yet picked up. | §8.2 step 1 |
| S3-C4 | The server accepts a `recoveryFp` change with or without a rotation; tools upload the rotation first. | §4.3 |
| S3-C5 | Account deletion keeps the tombstone and log of an in-grace vault whose log names other members. | §3.8, §3.11 |
| S3-C6 | Both recovery commits answer a session plus `recoveryPieceId`. | §9.3, §10 |
| S3-C8 | `400 cannot_target_self`; operator posture refused on the operator account. | §4.6, §9.2 |
| S3-K1 | A wrong password on a re-authenticated route is `403 reauth_failed`, never `401`. | §7.7 |
| SR-1 | Links: the server accepts `now < expiresAt <= now + maximum + 10 min` and stores the signed value as sent; the 5-minute minimum is the apps' rule. Device links: the server stores `min(expiresAt, now + 5 min)` as the `open` timeout and refuses only `expiresAt <= now`. Both answer `{expiresAt, ttlMs}`, and the countdown runs from `ttlMs`. | §6.6, §7.8, §8.2 |
| SR-2 | The sealed account state may be 1 MiB of base64, with the arithmetic; contacts and contact tombstones capped at 1,000; vault records batched. The device-link bundle keeps its 64 KiB and is filled most recent first. | §5.4, §8.3 |
| SR-3 | `PUT /recovery/self-setup` is re-authenticated, so a wrong key is `403 reauth_failed`; the session-less recovery routes keep `401`. One list of re-authenticated routes. | §7.8 |
| VR-1 | A `genesis` at `seq` 0 with zero `prev`, and other kinds at `seq` 1 or above, are template rules (`bad_statement`); position is `bad_seq` and `bad_prev` alone. | §3.2, §17 |
| VR-2 | A `genesis` signs itself: a signer that is not its `ownerKey` is `not_authorized`. | §3.3, §17 |
| VR-3 | A template's listed literals are a type: other values are `non_canonical`. | §2.2, §17 |
| VR-4 | `v` is an `int`; `v != 2` is `bad_statement` (since then `v == 3` is also taken for the two version-3 templates, §2.2). | §2.2, §17 |
| VR-5 | Origin edges: digit-only or `0x` last labels refused; labels 1–63 and hosts up to 253 characters; never the scheme's default port; `xn--` labels never decoded. | §2.2, §17 |
| VR-6 | `emailNorm` refuses any byte outside `0x21–0x7E` after the strip, and measures length after the strip. | §2.3, §17 |
| VR-7 | `email_b64n` must decode to an address that is already normal. | §2.2, §17 |
| VR-8 | Frame checks in spec 01's order: magic before length. | §2.4, §17 |
| VR-9 | Every per-purpose helper's signer failure is `signer_not_pinned_key`, with the key each one is handed; log, card and grants keep `not_authorized`. | §2.4, §17 |
| VR-10 | Card verdicts and order, with `instance_mismatch` in `wrong_vault`'s place and no `precondition`. | §2.4, §4.2 |
| VR-11 | Only the operator's own devices check the operator key, byte for byte against the Ed25519 key they derive from their own identity seed, never through its X25519 image: `operator_key_mismatch`. | §4.1, §2.4, §17 |
| VR-12 | Pinning verdicts: `card0_mismatch`, `instance_mismatch`, `card_not_in_chain`, `fork`, `withheld`. | §2.4, §17 |
| VR-13 | Genesis: one verdict per check, in order; all show the same copy. | §2.4, §5.2, §17 |
| VR-14 | Link verdicts for both sides, helper checks first. | §2.4, §6.8, §17 |
| VR-15 | Host JSON with another `purpose` is `unknown_format`. | §2.4, §6.4, §17 |
| VR-16 | Acceptance rows belong in `accountstate.json`. | §2.4, §17 |
| VR-17 | Checkpoint comparison (`ok`, `withheld`, `fork`) is a pure function with vector rows. | §2.4, §17 |
| IN-1 | Attachment upload: 600 an hour per account, beside its concurrent-upload cap. | §7.8 |
| IN-2 | Email links only to `https://andvaripass.com/help/<topic>/`, with six named topics. | §11.2 |

## 0. Decisions, scope and terms

### 0.1 Owner decisions (2026-11-14)

Rows W1 and W2 were written as **working assumptions**, because IF-2 needed an answer before it froze, so they followed the plan's recommendations (FI-B11). **The owner confirmed both on 2026-11-15 (§0.6).**

| # | Decision |
|---|---|
| A | Security events are signed by the acting account's identity key, in a per-vault log. Only the vault's owner can grant it. This reverses Q11: lifecycle proofs are no longer MACs. |
| B, C | Trust between two people is anchored by the link or QR itself: a secret the server never sees. Invites are created and shared from the apps. The server stops composing invite emails. |
| D | Join links and the product's public face live on the product's own origin, written `https://andvaripass.com` here (§0.3). The current instance moves to the new origin before launch. |
| E | The app stores wait for the whole plan: signed log, instance card, invites v2, public sign-up, device linking, recovery kit, native password change, guided self-host setup command, headless CLI. |
| Pivot: public instance | <ul><li>The existing server becomes a public instance: anyone may sign up with a verified email.</li><li>Accounts are capped in total (200 to start), and each account has a storage limit. The operator can raise the cap or close sign-up.</li><li>Individuals only: no households or tenancy inside an instance.</li><li>Recovery is the person's choice at sign-up: **private** (their own kit) by default; operator recovery only as an explicit opt-in, after a warning. **Amended by working assumption W1** (below): on the public instance there is no operator recovery, so sign-up there offers no choice, only the kit. The choice stays on private instances (§7.7).</li><li>The operator is a separate, operator-only account.</li><li>The first screen offers three equal choices.</li><li>Quiet launch (noindex), US only, minimum age 13, free, donations on the website only, one server run best-effort with tested off-site backups.</li><li>Automatic emails go through Microsoft Graph from one shared mailbox.</li></ul> |
| Clean break | No compatibility period; the owner's server and devices cut over together. Deleted rather than migrated:<ul><li>v1 seals;</li><li>MAC lifecycle proofs;</li><li>legacy review and re-issue;</li><li>typed 32-hex codes and the in-person affirmation;</li><li>v1 invite links;</li><li>server-composed invite emails.</li></ul> |
| Pace | The overhaul builds on `main` after the interim 0.30.0, in parallel parts with disjoint module ownership (§18). |
| W1 *(working assumption; confirmed by the owner 2026-11-15, §0.6)* | **Variant R on the public instance:** no operator recovery there (§7.7). IF-2 is written for R. Variant K for the public instance is the bounded delta of Appendix A, so switching is a known amount of work, not a redesign. |
| W2 *(working assumption; confirmed by the owner 2026-11-15, §0.6)* | **Q4 yes:** the operator may delete an account that has been disabled for at least 30 days, and the person is emailed (§9.2, §19). |

### 0.2 Accepted defaults (each open to owner veto)

| # | Default |
|---|---|
| F | The operator account's identity key signs the **instance card**. Each card update is signed by the previous card's key, so an operator handover is itself a signed update. |
| G | There is no admin role beside the operator. Each instance has exactly one operator account. |
| H | Device linking carries a session and the trust records, but not the vault key. The master password is typed once on the new device. |
| I | Optional per-account setting "new devices must be linked from one of mine". Off by default; may ship after launch. |
| J | No introductions (no transitive trust). |
| K | Recovery kit: the existing 256-bit secret in its 43-character form, a QR, and a printable kit. Confirmed by typing three random groups. |
| L | The vault named in the account genesis record is the personal vault, forever. It is never shared, and re-designation is gone. |
| M | The web client never binds identities. It does not create or accept links, does not approve device links, and does not sign instance cards (§13). |
| O | Store builds of the browser extension start with no server. |
| P | **Private (family) instances are first-class** (§7.1 `ANDVARI_INSTANCE_KIND=private`). On a private instance any member may create register links, so each family member is pinned to the person who invited them (§6.7), and the recovery copy is written for people who know each other (§7.7). **The operator account stays separate on every instance:** it is used once at setup and for rare card updates, signs in only on native apps or the CLI (§9.1), and is never anyone's everyday account. |

### 0.3 Names and origins (owner, 2026-11-14)

| Name | Value | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| Product origin | `https://andvaripass.com` | The public face: website, the scriptless join pages `/l` and `/d`, the app-association files, privacy, terms, support and donate. Served by a static host, **never** by an andvari server process (§6.1). |
| Public instance | `https://vault.andvaripass.com` | The public instance's API and web app (`ANDVARI_CANONICAL_ORIGIN`). The current instance moves here before launch. |
| Instance display name | "andvari" | What the apps show for the public instance; the card's `name`. |
| Product's full name | "andvaripass" | Used where a unique identifier is needed (domain, Android application id). The name people see stays "andvari". |
| Mail | `support@andvaripass.com` | One shared mailbox that sends the automatic mail and receives replies and support (§11.1). |

**Baked into store builds:**

- `PUBLIC_INSTANCE = { origin: "https://vault.andvaripass.com", instanceId, card0Hash, kind: "public" }` (§4.4), recorded when card 0 is signed at the cut-over;
- the join base `https://andvaripass.com`;
- the iOS associated domain `applinks:andvaripass.com`, whose association file lists the path `/l` only;
- the Android app-links host `andvaripass.com`, for the path `/l` only. **`/d` is not an app link** (§8.2 step 0, T2-B1).

**Android application id.** In the clean-break release it changes from `com.silencelen.andvari` to a new id under the new domain, in both the Play and the direct flavors.
- The new id is **`com.andvaripass.app`** (decided on 2026-11-15). The build records it as one constant, and the public constants (`spec/public-instance.json`) record it beside its certificate.
- The visible name stays "andvari", and the R-class `namespace` stays as it is.
- The interim 0.30.0 keeps the old id in its direct build, so the owner's phone updated in place. Its Google Play build already carries the new id and is on Play's internal test track (2026-11-16).
- **One key signs the new id in every channel** (owner, 2026-11-16, §0.6): the Play build and the direct build (the download, devstore, GitHub) are signed with the same new key, certificate SHA-256 `3C:49:4F:27:57:7F:C3:5D:EA:0F:2A:D7:AF:32:B1:D4:76:BB:70:A2:4F:B7:F3:04:6A:B5:0F:A6:A0:FC:F7:F1`, so a phone can move between channels without reinstalling. The existing release key signs the old id only. The build keeps this: the certificate's SHA-256 is recorded beside the application id in `spec/public-instance.json` (`android.releaseCertSha256`), and a release build of the new id compares the release keystore's certificate with it before anything is signed and fails on any other. A build without release signing is left unsigned and checks nothing.

**iOS becomes a new app (decided 2026-11-24):** the bundle id is `com.andvaripass.app`, with an App Group and a keychain group of its own, named after it, and a new App Store Connect record; the record of the app of 0.30.0 is renamed and kept.

### 0.4 Terms

| Term | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **instance** | One andvari server and its database. |
| **operator** | The single account with the `operator` role on an instance. It signs the instance card. |
| **member** | Any other account. |
| **contact** | A person whose identity key this account pinned through a link. |
| **link** | A connect, register or setup link (§6). |
| **device link** | A one-time pairing that adds a device to an account (§8). |
| **log** | A shared vault's signed history (§3). |
| **card** | The instance card (§4). |
| **genesis** | The account genesis record (§5). |
| **kit** | The recovery kit (§10). |

### 0.5 Out of scope

- Vault-key rotation (P6). Removal still does not revoke what a removed member copied (R7 unchanged).
- Federation or sharing across instances.
- Moving an account from one instance to another (export and import remains the path).
- Multiple operators on one instance.
- Households and tenancy.
- Sign-in and recovery proofs bound to the address dialed (a signature over a server nonce and the origin instead of a reusable key). It would remove the relay class that the pins and the Trust Gate guard against (R22, R36), and it replaces the login protocol: a later design.
- Being signed in to more than one instance at a time. An app talks to one instance at a time. Each instance keeps its own namespace on the device, and switching is explicit (§6.7, §12.3).

### 0.6 Owner decisions after the freeze (2026-11-15 to 2026-11-18)

Each row is the owner's own answer, with its date and time (PST), or a rule set while his answer is awaited, marked as such and open to his review; what it settles in this design is beside it.

| Date | Decision | What it settles |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-11-15 23:28 | Operator recovery is **not offered on the public instance**. | Working assumption W1 (§0.1) is confirmed: variant R on the public instance (§7.7). Appendix A stays as the record of variant K. |
| 2026-11-15 23:28 | Deleting accounts as operator: **yes, as assumed**. | Working assumption W2 (§0.1) is confirmed: the operator may delete an account disabled for at least 30 days, and the person is emailed (§9.2). |
| 2026-11-16 16:33 | When a handover makes someone's account the server's operator, on their phone: **stop using it at once, keep the copy locked, let the person remove it.** | §9.1 "On a phone", and §12.2 rule 7. |
| 2026-11-16 16:33 | Android: **Paparazzi, a test-only library**, so that every screen of the app can be drawn to an image on the build host and looked at. | The Android module may add it for its tests only; it ships in no build. Added on the integration branch: the plugin `app.cash.paparazzi` 1.3.5, on the unit tests' classpath only; the screens are drawn only when the build is asked to (`-PandvariScreens`), and the normal test run leaves them out. |
| 2026-11-16 16:34 | Desktop on Windows: **hide the whole andvari window from screenshots and screen recording, with a switch in Settings to allow capture.** | Built (2026-11-16): every window is created hidden, registered with one capture guard and only then shown. On Windows the guard calls `SetWindowDisplayAffinity` through the JNA library already in the project (no new dependency): `WDA_EXCLUDEFROMCAPTURE` first (from Windows 10 2004 the window is left out of every capture), else `WDA_MONITOR` (older Windows shows it black in captures); when both are refused, Settings says hiding is not active on this computer. The switch "allow screen capture" is off by default, kept per computer, and applies at once to every open window. On Linux (and macOS, for which the desktop app is not built) Settings says in one sentence that hiding is not possible there. Dialogs drawn inside the window are covered; the system's own file and print dialogs and the second-start message are separate windows and are not, and none of them shows vault content. |
| 2026-11-16 16:34 | Make the website's mail-retention sentence true in Microsoft 365: **yes, for the support mailbox only.** | §11.1. In the Microsoft 365 tenant that sends the mail, the shared mailbox `support@andvaripass.com`, and no other mailbox, has the Exchange retention policy "andvari support mailbox": sent mail is deleted 90 days after it was sent, all other mail one year after it arrived, junk after 30 days (the tenant's own tag). "Deleted" means moved to the mailbox's recoverable items, which Exchange keeps 14 days more. Each message counts from its own date, not from the end of its conversation. Exchange's own assistant skips a mailbox below 10 MB, which this one is, so until it grows the policy acts only when someone starts the assistant for that mailbox by hand. |
| 2026-11-16 19:45 | Android: **take the newer build of the encryption library (lazysodium-android, built for 16 KB memory pages) on the new app's line now**; 0.30.0 stays as it is; **a check on the owner's own phone comes before any release that carries it.** | Taken on the integration branch since 2026-11-17 03:09: lazysodium-android 5.2.0, whose native library is aligned for 16 KB memory pages. Its code needs a newer Java than the build machine had for the Android tests, and the owner decided on 2026-11-17 (01:51) that the build machine gets that newer Java beside the present one. So the Android app's unit tests run on Java 21, and every other build and test (the shared code, the server, the desktop app, the tools, every compile) stays on Java 17. **Before any release carries it, the one-tap encryption check on a real phone must pass on the build that carries it** (all of its rows, 27 of 27 on the build machine's own run); the check made on 2026-11-17 00:19 was of an earlier build that carried 5.1.0 and does not count for it. |
| 2026-11-16 19:47 | Android: the screen drawings are **not kept in the repository** as references a test compares against: **they are drawn on request, and a page of them is published per release.** | No image of a screen is committed, and no test compares a screen with a recorded image. The drawings come from the Paparazzi tests above. |
| 2026-11-16 21:00 | The new apps' screens: follow the current apps? **"Yes: Android now, and the desktop after it."** | The current apps are the reference for the structure of the new apps' screens: for a screen the app published today also has, the new Android app follows the published Android app's structure now, and the new desktop app follows the published desktop app's after it. |
| 2026-11-16 19:43 | Google Play: which key signs the Play app? **A new key, made for the Play app only, with a copy given to Play.** | The new application id (§0.3) is signed by a new key, never by the existing release key. |
| 2026-11-16 23:03 | The new app line: which key signs a build that does not come from Play? **"One app: the new line's direct build is signed with the same new key."** | §0.3 and §14.1 step 8. The new application id is signed by one key wherever the build comes from (Google Play, the direct download, devstore, GitHub), so the builds of the two channels can replace each other on a phone; certificate SHA-256 `3C:49:4F:27:57:7F:C3:5D:EA:0F:2A:D7:AF:32:B1:D4:76:BB:70:A2:4F:B7:F3:04:6A:B5:0F:A6:A0:FC:F7:F1`. The existing release key signs the current app's id only. A release build of the new id refuses any other signing key (§0.3). |
| 2026-11-16 23:03 | Two-step sign-in can be turned off from a signed-in session with the code alone; fix it in 0.30.0 too? **"Fix it on the new line only; cut 0.30.0 as it is."** | §7.8 and spec 05 R12: 0.30.0 keeps the weakness, as the released 0.29 line has it; the new line closes it. |
| 2026-11-16 23:04 | Google Play: the 0.30.0 draft rolled out to internal testing and installed? **"Done: it is installed from Play."** The separate upload key registered again? **"Requested."** | 0.30.0's Google Play build, under the new id (§0.3), is on Play's internal test track and installed from there. |
| 2026-11-17 00:18 | The new app's signing key kept in the owner's own safekeeping? **"Done: I hold a copy."** | The key that signs the new application id in every channel (§0.3) has a copy in the owner's own hands. |
| 2026-11-17 01:51 | The wording proposals for what the apps still show as placeholders: **"i take your proposals as written unless I object to one in further reviews."** | The proposals that are on his wording page are approved as written; he may object to one in a later review, and it then changes. A placeholder that has no entry on that page is not approved by this answer: it keeps its placeholder until a proposal for it is on the page. The approved wording names the second factor **"two-step sign-in"**, the name this design uses, and that is the product's one name for it; the web client and the extension say it too since 2026-11-17 04:26, and the name has an entry of its own on the wording page, marked new, so that he can object to it like to any other. **The approved words are in the apps since 2026-11-17 09:27**: they stand in place of the placeholders they cover in the shared code, the Android and desktop apps, the web app and the extension; a placeholder with no approved words yet stays a placeholder and stays marked. **Later changes** are made the same way, at any time: a new or changed proposal goes onto the wording page, counts as approved as written unless he objects, and its words are then put into the code in place of the placeholder, or of the earlier words, under the same key. Which keys still await his words is read from the code and shown on that page. The tests name a line by its key, never by a placeholder's text, so they hold whatever its words are. |

---

## 1. Adversaries and goals

| Adversary | Can |
|---|---|
| **T1 server**, which is the operator on an instance they run | Reads and changes everything stored. Withholds, delays, replays, reorders and forks what it serves. Registers accounts of its own. Serves the web client's code (T6). |
| **L link channel** | Reads, replaces or races a link or QR on its way between people. Examples: a shoulder-surfed QR, an intercepted message. |
| **E mailbox** | Reads the recipient's mail, or controls the sending tenant. |
| **A abuser** | Creates many accounts, floods codes, fills the cap. |
| **M member** | Holds keys for the vaults it is in, and colludes with T1. |
| **D device** | Holds a stolen or borrowed device, locked or unlocked (unchanged, spec 05 T3/T4). |

**Goals.**

- **G1.** No trust decision rests on a value only the server vouches for. A person's key is pinned only from a link that person's own app made, or from a device the account already trusts.
- **G2.** Only a vault's owner can change who holds that vault's key. Every membership, role, ownership and lifecycle change is attributable to the account that signed it, and an M+T1 coalition can forge none of them.
- **G3.** Two people verify each other **once**. The result reaches every device either of them links.
- **G4.** Nobody is asked a question they cannot answer: no raw fingerprints, no "which vault is personal", no "do you have a printed sheet".
- **G5.** Withholding and forking by T1 are detected where a device holds enough history to tell, and fail safe everywhere else.
- **G6.** The public instance admits strangers without a third-party CAPTCHA, bounds the damage they can do, and never reveals whether an email address has an account.
- **G7.** The operator's power over members is exactly what §9.2 lists, plus whatever T1 already has. An **honest** operator's every use of it is audited and emailed to the member. Audit and email are transparency for an honest operator, not controls against a hostile one: the operator is the host and can suppress both (T-R6).

---

## 2. Encoding foundations

### 2.1 Signed statement frame (unchanged layout)

New statements use the existing format-v2 frame (spec 01 §5.1) and the existing joint Ed25519 key derived from the identity seed. No new key and no fingerprint change.

```
offset  len  field
0       4    magic        61 76 73 02
4       1    purposeCode
5       32   signerPub    Ed25519 public key
37      64   signature    Ed25519 over M
101     N    statement    canonical JSON, printable ASCII, 2 <= N <= 1024

M = "andvari/v2|signed-statement" || 0x00 || frame[0..5) || signerPub || statement
```

**Purpose codes.** Domain separation comes from the purpose byte inside `M`. The statement's `"purpose"` literal MUST match the code.

| Code | Purpose literal | Carried as | Defined in |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x01 | `vault-grant` | sealed box to the recipient (unchanged template) | spec 01 §6, binding amended in §3.5 |
| 0x02 | `escrow-uvk` | sealed box to the instance recovery key (version 3 since 2026-11-16: the statement names the account's address) | spec 04 §3; used only by operator-recovery accounts (§7.7) |
| 0x03 | `vault-log` | in the clear | §3 |
| 0x04 | `instance-card` | in the clear | §4 |
| 0x05 | `account-genesis` | in the clear | §5 |
| 0x06 | `link-offer` | AEAD envelope under the link's offer key | §6 |
| 0x07 | `link-answer` | AEAD envelope under the link's answer key | §6 |
| 0x08 | `recovery-rotation` | in the clear, served with the card chain | §4.5 |
| 0x09 | `assisted-recovery` | sealed box to the requesting device's one-time key (version 3 since 2026-11-16: it names the address and carries the commit value; template in spec 04 §7) | §9.3 |
| 0x0A | `recovery-veto` | in the clear, posted to the server | §9.3 |
| others | — | — | reserved; a parser returns `bad_frame` |

**Signer binding.** A verifier is handed the key the context says must have signed (§3.3, §4.2, §5.2, §6.8), and that key comes in one of two forms.

- **An X25519 key** (vault log, grants, links, genesis in full clients; and, for 0x08 and 0x09, the instance recovery public key). The verifier requires `ed25519_pk_to_curve25519(signerPub) == that key`.
- **An Ed25519 key** (the instance card's `operatorSignKey`, and the genesis in the extension, §12.2). The verifier requires `signerPub == that key`, byte for byte.

Either way, `crypto_sign_verify_detached` over `M` follows. Each purpose gets its own verify helper, which cannot be called without that expected key. There is still no "sign any statement" API on an account. Each flow below owns its own builder.

**Frame hash.** `H(frame) = lowercase hex SHA-256 of the full frame bytes` (magic through statement). The frame is already domain-separated, so no extra prefix is used.

### 2.2 Canonical statement grammar

These rules are inherited unchanged from spec 01 §5.1:
- printable ASCII only;
- no nesting: a statement is one flat object, so a `[` anywhere, or a second `{`, is `non_canonical`, and an implementation refuses it **before** handing the text to any general JSON parser (a parser that recurses once per level can be made to overflow its stack by a statement of a thousand brackets, and that is a crash, not a verdict);
- exact key order per template, every key present exactly once, no optional keys;
- no whitespace;
- strict parse followed by a re-encode byte-compare;
- the existing value types `uuid`, `b64_16`, `b64_32`, `hex64` and `ms`.

**New value types:**

| Type | Grammar |
|---|---|
| `int` | A decimal integer `0 <= x < 2^53`: `0`, or `[1-9][0-9]*`. |
| `origin` | `https://` + host + optional `:port`, all lowercase.<ul><li>Host is a DNS name of at least two labels, each `[a-z0-9]([a-z0-9-]*[a-z0-9])?` and 1–63 characters long, joined by `.` with no trailing dot; the whole host is at most 253 characters (VR-5).</li><li>Internationalised names appear only in A-label (`xn--`) form. An `xn--` label is an ordinary label under this grammar: compared byte for byte, never decoded or checked as punycode, so no engine's IDNA tables decide anything (VR-5).</li><li>IP literals are not allowed. A host whose **last label is all digits**, or is `0x` followed only by hex digits, is refused, because URL parsers read such a host as an IPv4 address: `1.2.3`, `999.1.1.1` and `example.123` are all refused. A last label that is exactly `0x`, with no hex digit after it, is refused too (URL parsers read it as 0). Bracketed IPv6 already fails the label grammar (VR-5).</li><li>Port is `1..65535` without leading zeros, and never the scheme's default: `443` for https, `80` for http (VR-5).</li><li>The only plain-http forms are `http://localhost[:port]` and `http://127.0.0.1[:port]`, for development only.</li><li>Path, query, fragment and userinfo are never allowed.</li></ul>**This grammar is the single source** for every origin a client stores, dials or shows (R4). `OriginCanon`, the Trust Gate and `andvari setup` refuse any origin it cannot carry, with "andvari needs an https address with a domain name for this server." |
| `name` | Unpadded base64url of UTF-8 bytes, decoded length 1..64, re-encoding identically. The decoded text must be valid UTF-8 and contain none of these code points: U+0000–U+001F, U+007F–U+009F, U+00AD, U+061C, U+180E, U+200B–U+200F, U+2028–U+202E, U+2060–U+2064, U+2066–U+206F, U+FEFF, U+FFF9–U+FFFB. No leading or trailing U+0020. The list is explicit so that no engine's Unicode tables decide the outcome (R3). No normalisation is applied. Clients render it only as plain text. |
| `role` | `"writer"` or `"reader"`. |
| `hex64n` | A `hex64`, or the literal `"none"`. |
| `email_b64n` | Unpadded base64url of the ASCII bytes of an `emailNorm` (§2.3), decoded 3..254 bytes and re-encoding identically; or the literal `"none"` (N3). The decoded bytes must already be normal: `emailNorm` of them must equal them exactly, so an uppercase letter or a surrounding space inside is `non_canonical` (VR-7). |
| `v` | The `int` type. Strict-parse step 6 then requires `v == 2`, or `v == 3` for the two version-3 templates (`escrow-uvk` and `assisted-recovery`, above), else `bad_statement`. So `-1`, `02`, `2.0` and `"2"` are `non_canonical`, and `1` or `3` is `bad_statement` under a version-2 template (VR-4; spec 01 §5.1). |
| enumerated literals | Where a template lists literals (the log's `kind`, the card's and the setup offer's `"public"\|"private"`, the link offer's `"connect"\|"register"`), they are a type of their own: any other value is `non_canonical` (VR-3). |

A JSON value that fails its type is `non_canonical`. A template whose fields are well-formed but violate a rule of its section is `bad_statement`.

**The account-key texts a server serves are held to the same strictness** (rule of 2026-11-17, spec 01 §7): the salt, the wrapped account key, the sealed identity seed and the personal vault's wrap are taken only in their canonical base64 form, at every take, before anything of them is stored; one that is not is the server's fault, and the answer is refused as one that is not the session it must be. And a kept text is judged unreadable only by the decoder its opening uses, so a copy that opens is never told that it is damaged. The shared code keeps both (2026-11-17); its older account road (`SessionEntry`, the unlock ladder) takes served account-key texts only in canonical form too (2026-11-18, spec 01 §7), and never tells a kept copy that it is damaged: a kept key record that its reader cannot read, or whose cost is below the floor, counts as no kept keys (an unlock then needs the server), and keys that do not open fail the unlock as any other failure does.

### 2.3 Domain strings

All new labels live in the `andvari/v2|` namespace. Every HKDF is HKDF-SHA-256 with an empty salt (RFC 5869 default). Every AD is `|`-joined UTF-8 (spec 00).

| Use | Construction |
|---|---|
| Vault-key commitment | `HKDF(ikm=VK, info="andvari/v2\|vk-commit\|{vaultId}", L=32)`, written as lowercase hex |
| Shared-vault UVK wrap AD (owner wrap and member self-wrap) | `andvari/v2\|vk-shared\|{vaultId}\|{userId}\|{genesisHash}`, where `genesisHash = H(genesis frame)` (T-B3). The personal wrap keeps `andvari/v1\|vk-personal\|…`. |
| Link id | `b64url(HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|link\|id", L=16))` |
| Link offer key | `HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|link\|offer", L=32)`; envelope AD `andvari/v2\|link-offer\|{linkId}` |
| Link answer key | `HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|link\|answer", L=32)`; envelope AD `andvari/v2\|link-answer\|{linkId}` |
| Link register token | `token = b64url(HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|link\|register", L=32))`. The server stores `tokenHash = b64url(SHA-256(ASCII(token)))`, which is today's `ServerCrypto.hashToken` over the 43-character token string. |
| Code commitment (both kinds of link, T-B1) | `commit = hexLower(SHA-256("andvari/v2\|code-commit\|" + b64url(nonce)))`, where `nonce` is 32 CSPRNG bytes kept by the committing side until it reveals it |
| Link comparison code | `u32be(SHA-256("andvari/v2\|link\|code\|" + side + "\|" + linkId + "\|" + offerHash + "\|" + answerHash + "\|" + b64url(nonceA) + "\|" + b64url(nonceB))[0..4]) mod 10^6`, zero-padded to 6 digits, where `side` is the literal `creator` or `answerer`. **There are two codes, one per side:** the link's creator shows the `creator` code and the answerer shows the `answerer` code, and each app accepts as typed input only the OTHER side's code. `nonceA` is committed in the offer and revealed after the answer; `nonceB` travels in the answer (§6.11). |
| Link creator fingerprint | `F = b64url(SHA-256(raw 32 bytes of the creator's identityPub)[0..16])`, carried in the link's fragment (§6.1, T-B6) |
| Device-link id | `b64url(HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|device-link\|id", L=16))` |
| Device-link MAC key | `HKDF(ikm=s, info="andvari/v2\|device-link\|mac", L=32)`; MAC inputs in §8.2 |
| Device-link comparison code | `u32be(SHA-256("andvari/v2\|device-link\|code\|" + linkId + "\|" + nPub + "\|" + b64url(nonceN) + "\|" + b64url(nonceE))[0..4]) mod 10^6`. `nonceN` is committed in the claim and revealed after `nonceE` arrives (§8.2). |
| Device-link bundle key | `HKDF(ikm=UVK, info="andvari/v2\|device-link\|bundle\|" + linkId, L=32)`; envelope AD `andvari/v2\|device-link-bundle\|{linkId}` (T-B1) |
| Sign-up code check | `HMAC-SHA-256(signupSecret, "andvari/v2\|signup-code\|" + emailNorm + "\|" + code)`; `signupSecret` is a 32-byte server secret in the environment |
| Sign-up proof of work (optional) | `SHA-256("andvari/v2\|signup-pow\|" + emailNorm + "\|" + utcDate + "\|" + nonce)` |
| The keyed hash of a typed address (server records, since 2026-11-18) | `hexLower(HMAC-SHA-256(K, "andvari/v2\|address\|" + emailNorm))` with `K = HMAC-SHA-256(enumSecret, "andvari/v2\|address-record-key")`; `enumSecret` is the server's secret for its decoy answers (spec 02 §5, spec 03 §14.1) |
| Recovery-key signing key | `crypto_sign_seed_keypair(recovery seed)`. It is the same seed that yields the instance recovery X25519 key (spec 04 §1), held only offline in `recovery-cli`. Verifiers require `ed25519_pk_to_curve25519(signerPub)` to equal the recovery public key that the pinned card's `recoveryFp` names (T-B4, T-B5). |
| Assisted-recovery payload | Purpose 0x09 (§9.3), signed by the recovery-key signing key, then sealed with `crypto_box_seal` to the request key |
| Recovery request code | `hexLower(SHA-256("andvari/v2\|recovery-request\|code\|" + b64url(rPub)))[0..24]`, shown as six groups of four (96 bits, T-B5) |

**Encoding rules for every input above (R3):**

- **Inputs are strings.** Every HKDF info, MAC and hash input is the UTF-8 encoding of the string shown, and every component is ASCII.
- **Ids** (`vaultId`, `linkId`, `requestId`, `userId`) appear as their canonical text.
- **Keys and other byte values inside an input** appear as unpadded base64url text. For example, `nPub` in the device-link MAC and comparison code is the 43-character base64url of the 32-byte key.
- **Digests inside an input** are lowercase hex (`offerHash`, `answerHash`, and `hex SHA-256(sealedBundle)`, taken over the raw sealed bytes).
- **MAC outputs** travel as unpadded base64url of the 32 bytes.
- **`platform`** is one of `android`, `ios`, `windows`, `macos`, `linux`, `web`, `cli`, `extension`.
- **Proof of work.**
  - `utcDate` is `YYYY-MM-DD` (UTC).
  - `nonce` is a decimal integer `0 <= n < 2^53` with no leading zeros.
  - "Leading zero bits" are counted from the most significant bit of digest byte 0, then byte 1, and so on.
- **`emailNorm`.**
  - Strip ASCII white space (space, tab, CR, LF) from both ends.
  - Lowercase ASCII `A–Z` only: no Unicode case folding, no locale.
  - Refuse (`400 invalid_email`; in `encodings.json` the verdict `invalid_email`) any address that, **after the strip**, is shorter than 3 or longer than 254 octets, contains any byte outside `0x21–0x7E`, or does not have exactly one `@` with non-empty parts. So an interior space, a tab, any control character, DEL (`0x7F`) and any non-ASCII byte are all refused (VR-6).
  - After these steps the address must be `local@domain` with exactly one `@`: `local` is 1 to 64 characters of letters, digits and ``!#$%&'*+/=?^_`{|}~-`` with single dots between non-empty parts (a dot-atom: no quotes, no comments, no angle brackets, no spaces); `domain` is two or more labels under the host grammar of the `origin` type. Anything else has no `emailNorm`.

The AEAD envelope format is spec 02 §2, unchanged.

### 2.4 Verdict strings (the names the vector files use)

Every verifier stops at the first failure and returns exactly one of these strings, in the order given. Kotlin, TypeScript and the extension must agree on each (VR, the questions raised while the test vectors were written).

**Frame and strict parse, every purpose** (spec 01 §5.1). In order:
1. `bad_frame` if shorter than 4 bytes;
2. `unknown_format` for a wrong magic, including another version byte;
3. `bad_frame` for a length outside `103..1125`, a reserved purpose code, or a purpose other than the one the caller expects;
4. `non_canonical` (steps 2–5) and `bad_statement` (step 6, then each section's template rules).

The order is normative: a 50-byte frame with version byte `0x03` is `unknown_format`, because the magic is checked before the length (VR-8).

**Signer binding (VR-9).** Each per-purpose helper is handed the key the context says must have signed. When the signer does not map to it, the helper returns `signer_not_pinned_key`; a bad signature is `bad_signature`. The vault log (0x03), the card (0x04) and grants (0x01) return `not_authorized` instead, because their rules also check the acting account (§3.3, §3.5, §4.2).

| Purpose | Key the helper is handed |
|---|---|
| 0x05 genesis | the account's own seed-derived **Ed25519** signing key, compared byte for byte, on every client (each device of the account holds the seed). The server, which holds no seed, checks the signer against `identityPub` through its X25519 image and stores `signerPub`. A genesis signed under the negated key therefore registers and is then refused by every device of the account (`signer_not_pinned_key`), the same on all three implementations. |
| 0x06 link offer | the offer's `fromKey`, once it has passed the link's `F` (`creator_mismatch`) |
| 0x07 link answer | the answer's own `fromKey`: proof of possession, which the comparison code authenticates later |
| 0x08 rotation | the recovery key of the verifier's current fingerprint (its consent, or the card's); a `fromFp` that is not that fingerprint is `fp_mismatch`, checked right after the parse (the fingerprint is inside the statement) and before the signer |
| 0x09 assisted recovery | the recovery key that the pinned card's `recoveryFp` names |
| 0x0A veto | the account's stored identity key (the server verifies it) |

**Each flow, in order:**

| Flow | Verdicts |
|---|---|
| Vault log (§3.3) | the parse verdicts; `wrong_vault`; `bad_seq`; `bad_prev`; `not_authorized`; `bad_signature`; `precondition`; `fork`. A `genesis` is checked against its own `ownerKey` (VR-2). |
| Card chain (§4.2, VR-10) | the parse verdicts; `instance_mismatch` (an `instanceId` other than card 0's of the same chain, or, on the server, than its own); expectations from outside the chain (baked, kit, offer, pin) belong to the pinning row and are checked after the chain verifies; `bad_seq`; `bad_prev`; `not_authorized` (the signer is not byte-equal to the previous card's `operatorSignKey`, or card 0's own); `bad_signature`; then, only on the operator's own devices, `operator_key_mismatch` (VR-11). Cards have no `precondition`. |
| Pinning (§4.4, VR-12) | `origin_mismatch` first: the newest verified card's `origin` is not the origin the client dialed. Cards are public, so any server can serve another instance's genuine chain; without this check a look-alike address would pass the Trust Gate, a link offer, a kit or the CLI's `--trust-card` with the real instance's name and key. (Under an existing pin a changed origin is a move and is handled as one.) Screens that name a server show the dialed origin, whole. This holds after a move too: a device with no pin that dials an instance's OLD address, where the chain's newest card names the new one, gets `origin_mismatch`; the app may then offer to dial the origin that card names, and every check runs again from the start there (a kit's `card0Hash` still anchors it; a typed address passes the Trust Gate for the new origin). An empty chain is `withheld` for every root, the typed address included. A move met while a link is being opened under an existing pin is handled as a move first, and the link is opened again afterwards. Then `card0_mismatch` (card 0 does not hash to the baked or the kit's `card0Hash`); `instance_mismatch` (the baked `instanceId`); `card_not_in_chain` (a link offer's `cardHash`); `fork` (card 0 does not hash to the pin's `card0Hash`, or another hash stands at the pinned `seq`); `withheld` (the same chain ends below the pin: a state, not a stop). In that order, so: a baked root whose card 0 hash and `instanceId` both differ is `card0_mismatch`; a baked or kit root against an empty chain is `withheld`; an offer whose `cardHash` is in the chain under another `instanceId` than the offer's is `instance_mismatch`, and one whose `cardHash` is absent is `card_not_in_chain`; a pinned card against a chain of another `instanceId` is a `fork` (its card 0 differs). |
| Account keys at sign-in, unlock, kit recovery and device-link pickup (§5.2) | `no_genesis` (the account keys carry no genesis); the genesis verdicts of the next row; `identity_mismatch` (the served `identityPub` is not the key derived from the opened seed). All of them are shown as the identity fault. One name each on every client: an engine-private code such as `identity_pub_mismatch` is not a verdict string. |
| Account genesis (§5.2, VR-13) | the parse verdicts; `signer_not_pinned_key`; `bad_signature`; `identity_mismatch` (`identityKey` is not the seed-derived key); `user_mismatch`; `instance_mismatch`; `personal_vault_mismatch`. Every one shows the same `IDENTITY_MISMATCH` copy. |
| Opening a link (§6.8, VR-14) | `bad_link` (the URL does not parse); `link_unknown`, `link_expired` or `link_used` (the server's answer); `unopenable` (the offer does not open, or its envelope is larger than the cap of §6.6, which is refused before any key is derived); `unknown_format` and `kind_mismatch` (§6.4). `kind_mismatch` is returned as soon as it can be known: from the form alone (a signed frame where the server reported a host-minted kind, or host JSON where it reported `connect` or `register`) before any parse, from a signed offer's own `kind` right after its strict parse, and from a host offer's own `purpose` right after it is recognised (a `setup-offer` where the server reported `enrol`, or the reverse). A host offer that is not recognised is `non_canonical` or `unknown_format` first, whatever kind it seems to name. For a signed offer: the parse verdicts, `kind_mismatch`, `creator_mismatch`, `signer_not_pinned_key`, `bad_signature`. For host JSON: the parse verdicts, and `creator_mismatch` when `F` is not `-`. Then the field checks: `link_mismatch` (`linkId`), `origin_mismatch`, `offer_expired`, `reported_creator_mismatch` (the server-reported creator); then the pinning verdicts. A different key for a known contact is the `key_changed` prompt, not a verdict. |
| Processing an answer (§6.8, VR-14) | `unknown_link`; `unopenable` (also an envelope above the cap); the parse verdicts; `signer_not_pinned_key`; `bad_signature`; `link_mismatch`; `offer_mismatch` (`offerHash`); `misaddressed` (`to` or `toKey`); `reported_answerer_mismatch`. The answerer's check of the reveal is `bad_reveal`. |
| Host JSON offer (§6.4, VR-15) | printable ASCII and a JSON object with a string `purpose`, else `non_canonical`. "A JSON object" means RFC 8259 exactly: a bare token, `NaN`, a trailing comma or trailing bytes are `non_canonical`, and so are a key that appears twice at the top level (a repeated `purpose` included, whichever value comes last) and nesting deeper than 32 open containers. The depth is decided on the bytes, or by a parser that stops at the limit, before any general JSON parser sees the text: an offer of a thousand open brackets must be `non_canonical`, never a stack overflow (§2.2). Engines must not lean on a lenient library parser here: one that reads a bare token as a value would answer `unknown_format` where the others answer `non_canonical`; a `purpose` other than `setup-offer` and `enrol-offer` is `unknown_format`; then the template's own steps. |
| Rotation (§4.5) | the parse verdicts; `fp_mismatch`; `signer_not_pinned_key`; `bad_signature`; `instance_mismatch` (a statement made for another instance). |
| Veto (§9.3) | the parse verdicts; `signer_not_pinned_key`; `bad_signature`; `user_mismatch`; `request_mismatch`. |
| Assisted recovery (§9.3) | `unopenable` (the seal does not open); the parse verdicts; `signer_not_pinned_key`; `bad_signature`; `request_mismatch` (`instanceId`, `userId`, `requestId` or `requestKey`); `identity_mismatch` (the seed's key is not the served `identityPub`). |
| Device link (§8.2, §8.3) | **The existing device, on the claim it reads back:** `bad_claim` (a field outside its type: `nPub` a b64_32 that is not a low-order X25519 point, `name` the `name` type, `platform` one of §2.3's values, `commitN` a hex64, `mac` a b64_32); `bad_mac` (the claim MAC). **The existing device, on the revealed nonce:** `bad_reveal`. **The new device, before any password prompt:** `bad_mac` first (`approveMac` covers the raw sealed bytes, so it is checked before anything is opened or parsed); `outer_unopenable` (the sealed `outer` does not open); `bad_outer` (the plaintext is not exactly `{v, origin, userId, email}` with `v` the number 2, an `origin`, a `uuid`, and an email that is its own `emailNorm`); `origin_mismatch` (not the origin pinned in step 0). **The new device, after the pickup and the unlock:** the genesis verdicts first, because the genesis is verified when the device unlocks, as at every unlock, before the bundle is touched; then `bundle_unopenable` (an `inner` from another link fails here, on its AD); `bad_bundle` (the strict decode of §8.3: invalid UTF-8, more than 65,536 bytes, a key missing, extra or repeated at any level, `v` not the number 2, a value outside its type, more than 1,000 entries in `contacts` or `vaults`); `identity_mismatch` (the bundle's `userId`, `identityPub` or `personalVaultId` is not the session's, the seed-derived key or the verified genesis's); `pin_mismatch` (the bundle's `origin`, `instanceId` or card checkpoint disagrees with the pin of step 0, also when the chain still ends below the checkpoint after fetching forward: the device signs out and removes that pin). **Flow steps, each of which cancels the link:** `bad_link` (the `/d` URL does not parse), `claim_changed` (a later poll shows the existing device a claim that differs from the one it verified), `nonce_changed` (the new device is served a `nonceE` other than the first it held), `code_mismatch` (the digits typed into the existing device are not the code). `outer` and the bundle are **compact JSON**: no white space outside strings, strings escape only `"` and `\`, numbers in the `int` or `ms` text form, keys in any order, no key twice. |
| Small cases the vectors needed named | A recovery-kit text that does not parse is `bad_kit`. A setup link opened on an instance that already serves cards is `already_set_up`. In assisted recovery, an identity seed that does not open under the delivered UVK is `identity_mismatch` too, and a device that holds no `userId` (a fresh install) skips the `userId` comparison, because the seed's AD binds it. **Expiry is exclusive in every check against the current time:** a link, an offer, a code or a ticket is valid while `now < expiresAt` and has expired at `now = expiresAt`. The vault log's own rules compare an entry's signed `at`, not the clock, and stand as §3.3 has them (an `accept` is valid while `at <= expiresAt`). |
| Checkpoints (§3.9, §4.4, VR-17) | comparing a served chain with a held head gives `ok`, `withheld` (it ends below the head) or `fork` (another hash at the head's `seq`). It is one pure function shared by core and the web twin, with rows in `vaultlog.json` and `instancecard.json`. |
| Acceptance (§3.6 (d), VR-16) | an `accepted` record against the account's latest `addSeq` gives `accept`, `decline` or `ask`, with rows in `accountstate.json`. The extension reads `decision` only (FN-1). |

---

## 3. The vault log

Every **shared** vault has a log: an append-only, hash-chained list of signed entries. A personal vault has none (§5.3).

### 3.1 Templates (purpose 0x03)

Every entry starts with the same head, in this exact order:

```
{"v":2,"purpose":"vault-log","vaultId":<uuid>,"seq":<int>,"prev":<hex64>,"kind":<kind>,"actor":<uuid>,"at":<ms>
```
